刚果民主共和国的埃博拉病毒 暴发 有可能成为全球流行病。如何确保它不会

DRC’s Ebola outbreak has the potential to become a global pandemic. How to make sure it doesn’t
作者： Jia Kangbai
摘要中文翻译在 2014 年至 2016 年西非埃博拉病毒 暴发 期间进行的一项研究中,据报道超级传播者对持续蔓延的 传播 事件负有超过 60% 的责任。鉴于其速度、规模和范围,2026 年中期在刚果民主共和国爆发的 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒病 可能会成为全球流行病。我是一名传染病流行病学家,拥有在我的国家塞拉利昂管理 2014-2016 年埃博拉病毒 暴发 的经验。汲取塞拉利昂的教训并观看了 2026 年中期刚果民主共和国的 暴发,我得出的结论是,它有可能成为全球流行病。造成这种情况的原因有很多。阻止已经占领大片地理区域的快速传播的 暴发 是很困难的。暴发 的速度使得量化其传播的真实规模变得具有挑战性。很难每天衡量其真实指标,例如 发病率(检测到的病例数)和病死率(检测到的病例中死亡的比例或百分比)。2013-2016年西非几内亚、利比里亚和塞拉利昂的埃博拉暴发累计病例在正式宣布后平均用了四个月的时间才达到1000例。在刚果民主共和国 暴发 花了不到两个月的时间就达到了同样的数字。这些统计数据是 暴发 申报后的统计数据。这个暴发在被正式检测到之前就开始了很长时间,这意味着传播和死亡事件已经发生但未被捕获。只有在正式宣布后出现的埃博拉病例才被记录在案。这意味着一线卫生工作者必须应对 治疗、监测 和接触者追踪方面大量积压的埃博拉病例。鉴于其目前的规模和速度,需要采取以下行动:...
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究探讨了刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 的 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 升级为全球大流行的可能性,强调需要改进 暴发 的管理和准备。
设计与对象
该研究是对刚果民主共和国2026年本迪布焦埃博拉暴发的回顾性分析,并将其与2014-2016年西非埃博拉暴发进行比较。它重点关注刚果民主共和国暴发期间的流行病学特征、传播动态和面临的公共卫生挑战。
核心方法
这些方法包括回顾 2014-2016 年西非埃博拉病毒 暴发 的历史数据,并分析当前刚果民主共和国 暴发 的进展,包括达到 1,000 例病例的时间、超级传播者的作用以及诊断限制和社区参与的影响。
主要结果
刚果民主共和国 暴发 在正式宣布后不到两个月的时间里就达到了 1,000 例,而西非 暴发 则用了四个月的时间。据报道,西非地区 60% 以上的 传播 是由超级传播者造成的,而感染者的年龄是超级传播的关键因素。
解释与创新
作者将本迪布焦病毒在刚果民主共和国的迅速传播解释为全球流行病的重大风险,并强调需要尽早采取持续的公共卫生干预措施。新颖之处在于强调了 DRC 暴发 与之前的产品相比的独特挑战。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,迫切需要实施强有力的 监测、社区参与和资助策略,以防止本迪布焦埃博拉 暴发 升级为全球大流行。
局限与证据强度
该研究依赖历史数据和观察报告,这可能会引入偏差并限制其结论的可信度。由于缺乏实时数据并且依赖 暴发 后的统计数据,证据并不充分。

DRC’s Ebola outbreak has the potential to become a global pandemic. How to make sure it doesn’t

Authors: Jia Kangbai
Original abstract (English)
In one study conducted during the 2014-2016 west Africa Ebola outbreak, super-spreaders were reported to have been responsible for more than 60% of sustained onward transmission. Given its speed, scale and scope, the Bundibugyo Ebola virus disease that broke out in the Democratic Republic of Congo in mid-2026 could become a global epidemic. I am an infectious disease epidemiologist with experience in managing the 2014-2016 Ebola outbreak in my country, Sierra Leone. Drawing lessons from Sierra Leone and having watched the mid-2026 outbreak in the DRC, I conclude that it risks become a global epidemic. There are a number of reasons for this. Preventing a fast-spreading outbreak that has already taken over a large geographical region is difficult. The speed of the outbreak makes it challenging to even quantify the true scale of its spread. It’s hard to measure its true metrics, like incidence (the number of cases detected) and case fatality rates (the proportion or percentage deaths among the detected cases) on a daily basis. It took on average four months for the cumulative 2013-2016 west Africa Ebola outbreak cases in Guinea, Liberia and Sierra Leone to reach 1,000 after official declaration. In the DRC outbreak it took less than two months to reach that same number. These statistics are post-outbreak declaration statistics. This outbreak started a long time before it was officially detected, which implies transmissions and deaths have occurred that are not being captured. Only those Ebola cases that presented after its official declaration have been documented. This implies that frontline health workers have to contend with huge backlogs of Ebola cases for treatment, surveillance and contact tracing. Given its current scale and speed, the following actions are needed:...
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addresses the potential for the Bundibugyo Ebola virus disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) to escalate into a global pandemic, highlighting the need for improved outbreak management and preparedness.
Design and population
The study is a retrospective analysis of the 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak in the DRC, comparing it to the 2014-2016 West Africa Ebola outbreak. It focuses on the epidemiological characteristics, transmission dynamics, and public health challenges faced during the DRC outbreak.
Core methods
The methods involve reviewing historical data from the 2014-2016 West Africa Ebola outbreak and analyzing the current DRC outbreak's progression, including the time to reach 1,000 cases, the role of super-spreaders, and the impact of diagnostic limitations and community engagement.
Main results
The DRC outbreak reached 1,000 cases in less than two months after official declaration, compared to four months in the West Africa outbreak. Super-spreaders were reported to be responsible for over 60% of transmission in the West Africa outbreak, and the age of infected individuals was a key factor in super-spreading.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret the rapid spread of the Bundibugyo virus in the DRC as a significant risk for a global epidemic, emphasizing the need for early and sustained public health interventions. The novelty lies in highlighting the unique challenges of the DRC outbreak compared to previous ones.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the urgency of implementing robust surveillance, community engagement, and funding strategies to prevent the Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak from escalating into a global pandemic.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study relies on historical data and observational reports, which may introduce bias and limit the confidence in its conclusions. The evidence is moderate due to the lack of real-time data and the reliance on post-outbreak statistics.

本迪布焦埃博拉病毒在乌干达和刚果民主共和国的重新出现:流行病学驱动因素、应对策略以及对全球卫生安全的影响。

The Re-Emergence of Bundibugyo Ebolavirus in Uganda and the Democratic Republic of Congo: Epidemiological Drivers, Response Strategies, and Implications for Global Health Security.
作者： Gerald Olwit, Ivy Bochaberi Ombongi, Henon Ataryeba, Andrea Nantongo, Babirye Shamim
摘要中文翻译本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 (BDBV) 是正埃博拉病毒属(丝状病毒科)中研究最少的物种之一,尽管它能够引起严重的 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 和大量的 死亡率。BDBV 于 2007 年至 2008 年在乌干达西部本迪布焦区首次发现(报告 149 例病例,37 例死亡;病死率 [CFR] 约 25-36%),2012 年在刚果民主共和国(刚果民主共和国)东方省再次出现(57-59 例病例,29-34 例死亡;CFR) 34-58%),然后于 2026 年 4 月至 5 月在刚果民主共和国伊图里省重新出现。截至 2026 年 8 月 11 日,第三次 暴发 在刚果民主共和国五个省份和乌干达已增加到 4566 例实验室确诊病例和 2128 例死亡(CFR ≈ 47%),成为有记录以来规模最大、增长最快的 BDBV 流行病和第二大埃博拉家族暴发 总体而言。本叙述性综述不是系统综述或荟萃分析,它总结了来自 WHO、非洲 CDC、美国 CDC、ECDC 和国家卫生部的同行评审文献、预印本和官方情况报告,这些报告是通过 PubMed、Scopus、Web of Science、Google Scholar 和 Embase 确定的,从成立到 2026 年 8 月 12 日,以检查 BDBV 历史 进化、病毒学和 发病机制、驱动因素重新出现、监测 和反应、治疗和 疫苗 差距以及全球健康安全影响。2026 年 暴发 在刚果民主共和国东部的冲突和大规模流离失所中展开,其特点是基本传染数估计为 1.4-2.1(中心估计为 1.71)、医护人员感染比例过高(刚果民主共和国确诊病例为 7.2%,乌干达为 20%),并且持续缺乏获得许可的 BDBV 特异性 疫苗 或治疗药物。研究结果强调需要持续的基因组和生态 监测、分散的快速诊断、广泛保护的泛丝状病毒 疫苗、冲突敏感的应对策略以及加强乌干达-刚果民主共和国的合作。由于正在进行的 暴发 的证据基础仍处于初步阶段,因此应谨慎解释研究结果,并随着进一步同行评审数据的出现而重新审视研究结果。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本迪布焦埃博拉病毒 (BDBV) 是正埃博拉病毒属(丝状病毒科)中研究最少的物种之一,尽管它能够引起严重的 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 和大量的 死亡率。
证据基础与方法
本叙述性综述不是系统综述或荟萃分析,它总结了来自 WHO、非洲 CDC、美国 CDC、ECDC 和国家卫生部的同行评审文献、预印本和官方情况报告,这些报告是通过 PubMed、Scopus、Web of Science、Google Scholar 和 Embase 确定的,从成立到 2026 年 8 月 12 日,以检查 BDBV 历史 进化、病毒学和 发病机制、驱动因素重新出现、监测 和反应、治疗和 疫苗 差距以及全球健康安全影响。
共识与结论
2026 年 暴发 在刚果民主共和国东部的冲突和大规模流离失所中展开,其特点是基本传染数估计为 1.4-2.1(中心估计为 1.71)、医护人员感染比例过高(刚果民主共和国确诊病例为 7.2%,乌干达为 20%),并且持续缺乏获得许可的 BDBV 特异性 疫苗 或治疗药物。
争议与缺口
由于正在进行的 暴发 的证据基础仍处于初步阶段,因此应谨慎解释研究结果,并随着进一步同行评审数据的出现而重新审视研究结果。
科研与实践启示
研究结果强调需要持续的基因组和生态 监测、分散的快速诊断、广泛保护的泛丝状病毒 疫苗、冲突敏感的应对策略以及加强乌干达-刚果民主共和国的合作。

The Re-Emergence of Bundibugyo Ebolavirus in Uganda and the Democratic Republic of Congo: Epidemiological Drivers, Response Strategies, and Implications for Global Health Security.

Authors: Gerald Olwit, Ivy Bochaberi Ombongi, Henon Ataryeba, Andrea Nantongo, Babirye Shamim
Original abstract (English)
Bundibugyo ebolavirus (BDBV) is one of the least studied species within the genus Orthoebolavirus (family Filoviridae), despite its capacity to cause severe Ebola virus disease (EVD) with substantial mortality. First identified during a 2007-2008 outbreak in Bundibugyo District, western Uganda (149 reported cases, 37 deaths; case-fatality rate [CFR] approximately 25-36%), BDBV re-emerged in 2012 in Orientale Province, Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) (57-59 cases, 29-34 deaths; CFR 34-58%), before resurfacing in Ituri Province, DRC, in April-May 2026. By 11 August 2026, this third outbreak had grown to 4566 laboratory-confirmed cases and 2128 deaths (CFR ≈ 47%) across five DRC provinces and Uganda, becoming the largest, fastest-growing BDBV epidemic on record and the second-largest Ebola-family outbreak overall. This narrative review, not a systematic review or meta-analysis, summarizes peer-reviewed literature, preprints, and official situation reports from WHO, Africa CDC, US CDC, ECDC, and national health ministries, identified through PubMed, Scopus, Web of Science, Google Scholar, and Embase from inception to 12 August 2026, to examine BDBV historical evolution, virology and pathogenesis, drivers of re-emergence, surveillance and response, therapeutic and vaccine gaps, and global health security implications. The 2026 outbreak, unfolding amid conflict and mass displacement in eastern DRC, has been marked by an estimated basic reproduction number of 1.4-2.1 (central estimate 1.71), disproportionate infection among healthcare workers (7.2% of confirmed cases in DRC, 20% in Uganda), and the continued absence of licensed BDBV-specific vaccines or therapeutics. Findings underscore the need for sustained genomic and ecological surveillance, decentralized rapid diagnostics, broadly protective pan-filovirus vaccines, conflict-sensitive response strategies, and strengthened Uganda-DRC collaboration. Because the evidence base for the ongoing outbreak remains preliminary, findings should be interpreted cautiously and revisited as further peer-reviewed data emerge.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Bundibugyo ebolavirus (BDBV) is one of the least studied species within the genus Orthoebolavirus (family Filoviridae), despite its capacity to cause severe Ebola virus disease (EVD) with substantial mortality.
Evidence base and method
This narrative review, not a systematic review or meta-analysis, summarizes peer-reviewed literature, preprints, and official situation reports from WHO, Africa CDC, US CDC, ECDC, and national health ministries, identified through PubMed, Scopus, Web of Science, Google Scholar, and Embase from inception to 12 August 2026, to examine BDBV historical evolution, virology and pathogenesis, drivers of re-emergence, surveillance and response, therapeutic and vaccine gaps, and global health security implications.
Consensus and conclusions
The 2026 outbreak, unfolding amid conflict and mass displacement in eastern DRC, has been marked by an estimated basic reproduction number of 1.4-2.1 (central estimate 1.71), disproportionate infection among healthcare workers (7.2% of confirmed cases in DRC, 20% in Uganda), and the continued absence of licensed BDBV-specific vaccines or therapeutics.
Controversies and gaps
Because the evidence base for the ongoing outbreak remains preliminary, findings should be interpreted cautiously and revisited as further peer-reviewed data emerge.
Research and practice implications
Findings underscore the need for sustained genomic and ecological surveillance, decentralized rapid diagnostics, broadly protective pan-filovirus vaccines, conflict-sensitive response strategies, and strengthened Uganda-DRC collaboration.

对刚果民主共和国伊图里省 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒病 (BDBV) 暴发 的实地调查:暴发 控制的挑战、策略和优先行动——暴发 调查回顾

Field Investigation of the Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease (BDBV) Outbreak in Ituri Province, Democratic Republic of the Congo: Challenges, Strategies, and Priority Actions for Outbreak Control—An Outbreak Investigation Review
作者： Muambangu Jean Paul Milambo, Christian Ngandu
摘要中文翻译背景:2026 年发生在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 东部、以伊图里省为中心的本迪布焦 暴发 (BDBV) 的 暴发,是自 2007 年在乌干达发现以来记录的最大的由本迪布焦埃博拉病毒引起的 暴发。暴发 是在以武装冲突、人口流离失所、采矿相关等为特征的复杂人道主义环境中演变的。移民、卫生系统薄弱、人口流动性大,以及错误信息和公众信任度下降的信息流行环境。我们进行了现场调查,以评估流行病学、操作、实验室、感染预防和控制 (IPC)、社区参与、风险沟通和信息流行病管理挑战,并确定加强 暴发 控制的优先干预措施。方法:2026 年 6 月 12 日至 15 日在布尼亚、鲁万帕拉卫生区和伊图里省公共卫生实验室进行了快速现场评估。通过直接观察、审查 监测 和实验室报告、卫生设施评估、利益相关者访谈以及 暴发 响应指标分析来收集数据。评估了流行病学趋势、监测 绩效、实验室能力、临床护理、IPC 活动、后勤、风险沟通、社区参与和信息流行病管理方法。结果:截至 2026 年 7 月 12 日,暴发 已导致 1926 例实验室确诊病例和 702 例死亡,相当于受影响省份的总体病死率 (CFR) 为 36.4%。伊图里省仍然是震中,占确诊病例的 90.8% (1705/1877) 和报告死亡人数的 85.5% (577/675)。在过去 24 小时内,报告了 53 例新确诊病例和 30 例死亡,其中包括 20 例社区死亡(66.7%),凸显了检测、转诊和获得护理方面的持续延误。监测系统发现了766个警报,其中678个(88.5%)被调查,导致235个疑似病例。接触者追踪仍然是一个重大挑战,只有 64.4% (4171/6475) 的注册接触者成功追踪,低于建议的 ≥95% 目标。实验室活动包括检测 137 份样本,其中 29 份呈阳性,总体阳性率为 21.2%。分散式分子诊断平台改善了检测的机会;然而,数据不一致、调查延迟和结果分类方面的差距影响了应对监测。主要的运营挑战包括 治疗 能力有限、埃博拉 治疗 中心占用率高、训练有素的人员和 IPC 用品短缺、影响响应团队的不安全状况以及新受影响地区的准备不足。社区抵制、对埋葬团队的攻击、前线响应人员的拘留、错误信息和谣言导致了寻求护理的延迟、对公共卫生措施的接受度降低以及与接触者追踪的不完全合作。风险沟通和社区参与工作受到外展能力有限、语言障碍、信任度低以及谣言检测和信息流行病应对系统不足的限制。结论:刚果民主共和国东部正在发生的 BDBV 暴发 表明,在受冲突影响和社会复杂的环境中控制埃博拉病毒 传播 非常困难。持续的 传播、社区死亡、地理扩张和运营限制凸显了加强 传播、接触者追踪、实验室系统、IPC 能力、临床护理以及综合风险沟通和信息流行病管理策略的迫切需要。通过以社区为中心的方法、积极主动的错误信息管理以及值得信赖的当地参与者的参与来建立信任,对于加快 暴发 的遏制和加强整个大湖地区的准备工作至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 东部以伊图里省为中心的本迪布焦 暴发 (BDBV) 2026 年 暴发 是自 2007 年在乌干达发现以来记录的最大的由本迪布焦埃博拉病毒引起的 暴发。现场调查旨在评估流行病学、操作、实验室、感染预防和控制 (IPC)、社区参与、风险沟通和信息流行病管理挑战,并确定优先干预措施以加强 暴发 控制。
证据基础与方法
2026 年 6 月 12 日至 15 日期间,在布尼亚、鲁万帕拉卫生区和伊图里省公共卫生实验室进行了快速现场评估。通过直接观察、审查 监测 和实验室报告、卫生设施评估、利益相关者访谈以及 暴发 响应指标分析来收集数据。
共识与结论
截至 2026 年 7 月 12 日,暴发 已导致 1926 例实验室确诊病例和 702 例死亡,相当于受影响省份的总体病死率 (CFR) 为 36.4%。伊图里省仍然是震中,占确诊病例的 90.8% (1705/1877) 和报告死亡人数的 85.5% (577/675)。风险沟通和社区参与工作受到外展能力有限、语言障碍、信任度低以及谣言检测和信息流行病应对系统不足的限制。
争议与缺口
接触者追踪仍然是一个重大挑战,只有 64.4% (4171/6475) 的注册接触者成功追踪,低于建议的 ≥95% 目标。主要的运营挑战包括 治疗 能力有限、埃博拉 治疗 中心占用率高、训练有素的人员和 IPC 用品短缺、影响响应团队的不安全状况以及新受影响地区的准备不足。数据不一致、调查延迟和结果分类中的差距影响了反应监测。
科研与实践启示
持续的 传播、社区死亡、地理扩张和运营限制凸显了加强 传播、接触者追踪、实验室系统、IPC 能力、临床护理以及综合风险沟通和信息流行病管理策略的迫切需要。通过以社区为中心的方法、积极主动的错误信息管理以及值得信赖的当地参与者的参与来建立信任,对于加快 暴发 的遏制和加强整个大湖地区的准备工作至关重要。

Field Investigation of the Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease (BDBV) Outbreak in Ituri Province, Democratic Republic of the Congo: Challenges, Strategies, and Priority Actions for Outbreak Control—An Outbreak Investigation Review

Authors: Muambangu Jean Paul Milambo, Christian Ngandu
Original abstract (English)
Background: The 2026 outbreak of Bundibugyo Ebola virus disease (BDBV) in eastern Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), centered in Ituri Province, represents the largest documented outbreak caused by Bundibugyo ebolavirus since its discovery in Uganda in 2007. The outbreak evolved within a complex humanitarian setting characterized by armed conflict, population displacement, mining-related migration, weak health systems, extensive population mobility, and an infodemic environment marked by misinformation and reduced public trust. We conducted a field investigation to assess epidemiological, operational, laboratory, infection prevention and control (IPC), community engagement, risk communication, and infodemic management challenges and identify priority interventions to strengthen outbreak control. Methods: A rapid field assessment was conducted between 12–15 June 2026 in Bunia, Rwampara Health Zone, and the Ituri Provincial Public Health Laboratory. Data were collected through direct observation, review of surveillance and laboratory reports, health facility assessments, stakeholder interviews, and analysis of outbreak response indicators. Epidemiological trends, surveillance performance, laboratory capacity, clinical care, IPC activities, logistics, risk communication, community engagement, and infodemic management approaches were evaluated. Results: As of 12 July 2026, the outbreak had resulted in 1926 laboratory-confirmed cases and 702 deaths, corresponding to an overall case fatality rate (CFR) of 36.4% across affected provinces. Ituri Province remained the epicenter, accounting for 90.8% of confirmed cases (1705/1877) and 85.5% of reported deaths (577/675). During the preceding 24 h, 53 new confirmed cases and 30 deaths were reported, including 20 community deaths (66.7%), highlighting persistent delays in detection, referral, and access to care. Surveillance systems identified 766 alerts, of which 678 (88.5%) were investigated, resulting in 235 suspected cases. Contact tracing remained a major challenge, with only 64.4% (4171/6475) of registered contacts successfully followed, below the recommended ≥95% target. Laboratory activities included testing of 137 specimens, with 29 positive results and an overall positivity rate of 21.2%. Decentralized molecular diagnostic platforms improved access to testing; however, data inconsistencies, delayed investigations, and gaps in outcome classification affected response monitoring. Major operational challenges included limited treatment capacity, high occupancy of Ebola treatment centres, shortages of trained personnel and IPC supplies, insecurity affecting response teams, and insufficient preparedness in newly affected areas. Community resistance, attacks on burial teams, detention of frontline responders, misinformation, and rumors contributed to delayed care-seeking, reduced acceptance of public health measures, and incomplete cooperation with contact tracing. Risk communication and community engagement efforts were constrained by limited outreach capacity, language barriers, low trust, and inadequate systems for rumor detection and infodemic response. Conclusions: The ongoing BDBV outbreak in eastern DRC demonstrates the difficulty of controlling Ebola transmission in conflict-affected and socially complex settings. Sustained transmission, community deaths, geographic expansion, and operational constraints highlight the urgent need to strengthen surveillance, contact tracing, laboratory systems, IPC capacity, clinical care, and integrated risk communication and infodemic management strategies. Building trust through community-centered approaches, proactive misinformation management, and engagement of trusted local actors will be essential to accelerate outbreak containment and strengthen preparedness across the Great Lakes region.

Scope and question
The 2026 outbreak of Bundibugyo Ebola virus disease (BDBV) in eastern Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), centered in Ituri Province, represents the largest documented outbreak caused by Bundibugyo ebolavirus since its discovery in Uganda in 2007. The field investigation aimed to assess epidemiological, operational, laboratory, infection prevention and control (IPC), community engagement, risk communication, and infodemic management challenges and identify priority interventions to strengthen outbreak control.
Evidence base and method
A rapid field assessment was conducted between 12–15 June 2026 in Bunia, Rwampara Health Zone, and the Ituri Provincial Public Health Laboratory. Data were collected through direct observation, review of surveillance and laboratory reports, health facility assessments, stakeholder interviews, and analysis of outbreak response indicators.
Consensus and conclusions
As of 12 July 2026, the outbreak had resulted in 1926 laboratory-confirmed cases and 702 deaths, corresponding to an overall case fatality rate (CFR) of 36.4% across affected provinces. Ituri Province remained the epicenter, accounting for 90.8% of confirmed cases (1705/1877) and 85.5% of reported deaths (577/675). Risk communication and community engagement efforts were constrained by limited outreach capacity, language barriers, low trust, and inadequate systems for rumor detection and infodemic response.
Controversies and gaps
Contact tracing remained a major challenge, with only 64.4% (4171/6475) of registered contacts successfully followed, below the recommended ≥95% target. Major operational challenges included limited treatment capacity, high occupancy of Ebola treatment centres, shortages of trained personnel and IPC supplies, insecurity affecting response teams, and insufficient preparedness in newly affected areas. Data inconsistencies, delayed investigations, and gaps in outcome classification affected response monitoring.
Research and practice implications
Sustained transmission, community deaths, geographic expansion, and operational constraints highlight the urgent need to strengthen surveillance, contact tracing, laboratory systems, IPC capacity, clinical care, and integrated risk communication and infodemic management strategies. Building trust through community-centered approaches, proactive misinformation management, and engagement of trusted local actors will be essential to accelerate outbreak containment and strengthen preparedness across the Great Lakes region.

跨境本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 期间检测、通知和响应及时性的早期行动审查,乌干达,2026 年

Early Action Review of Detection, Notification, and Response Timeliness during Cross-Border Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak, Uganda, 2026
作者： Joshua Kayiwa, Mudarshiru Bbuye, Ritah Atuhaire, Doreen Nyakato, Mathew Tut, Bayad Nozad, Jovan Baryamujura, Bernard Lubwama, Solome Okware, Pamela Kajumba
摘要中文翻译第 32 卷,第 10 期 — 2026 年 10 月 摘要 跨境本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 期间检测、通知和响应及时性的早期行动审查,乌干达,2026 建议引用本文 摘要 本迪布焦病毒病 (BVD),一种没有获得许可的 疫苗 或治疗药物的 埃博拉病毒 物种,于 5 月重新出现2026年作为跨境暴发在乌干达和刚果民主共和国。在 2026 年 7 月 8 日至 9 日为期 2 天的研讨会期间,我们使用 7-1-7 框架(7 天检测、1 天通知、7 天完成早期响应行动)对 暴发 响应进行了早期行动审查,以评估及时性并确定 9 个响应支柱的瓶颈和推动因素。世卫组织建议对所有具有公共卫生意义的 暴发 进行早期行动审查 (EAR),这是一种灵活、结构化的方法,用于评估及时性并使用 7-1-7 工具识别瓶颈和促成因素 (22)。在本文中,我们介绍了该审查的过程、结果和经验教训。研究设计和背景 我们使用 WHO 的标准化 EAR 方法和 7-1-7 评估工具进行了回顾性、多支柱规划审查 (22)。免责声明:早期发布的文章不被视为最终版本。任何更改将在文章正式发布当月反映在在线版本中。乌干达于2026年5月15日宣布其暴发;截至7月8日,该国已报告确诊病例20例(其中输入性病例15例,本土传播病例5例),病死率为15%。乌干达实现了全部 3 个目标:6 天内检测、不到 1 天内通知、2 天内完成响应。临床怀疑度低、跨境数据共享差距、分散的数字系统和社区参与延迟是常见的瓶颈;英石...
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
此次审查的重点是 2026 年乌干达和刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 跨境本迪布焦病毒病 暴发 期间的检测、通知和响应及时性。核心问题是 暴发 响应是否满足及时检测、通知和响应行动的 7-1-7 框架目标。
证据基础与方法
证据基础包括 2026 年 7 月 8 日至 9 日举行的为期 2 天的研讨会,使用 7-1-7 框架评估 暴发 响应及时性。该审查评估了 9 个响应支柱的绩效,并确定了瓶颈和推动因素。该方法基于对 暴发 响应数据和利益相关者输入的系统审查。
共识与结论
乌干达实现了所有三个 7-1-7 目标:6 天内检测、<1 天内通知、2 天内完成响应。此次审查凸显了暴发的跨境性质,20例确诊病例中有15例来自刚果民主共和国。它还确定了关键的推动因素,例如强大的领导和协调结构,以及低临床怀疑和分散的数字系统等瓶颈。
争议与缺口
审查指出,报告的 BVD 病死率通常低于扎伊尔埃博拉病毒。然而,关于跨境监测和应对策略有效性的证据有限。此外,审查还确定了国家之间数据共享方面的差距以及改善社区参与的必要性。
科研与实践启示
审查强调需要在乌干达-刚果民主共和国走廊沿线进行持续的联合 监测 和协调筛查。它还强调了加强数字系统、改善临床怀疑和加强社区参与以预防未来 暴发 的重要性。候选 BVD 特异性 疫苗 和治疗方法正在接受 WHO 主导的优先排序,这可能会为未来的公共卫生策略提供信息。

Early Action Review of Detection, Notification, and Response Timeliness during Cross-Border Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak, Uganda, 2026

Authors: Joshua Kayiwa, Mudarshiru Bbuye, Ritah Atuhaire, Doreen Nyakato, Mathew Tut, Bayad Nozad, Jovan Baryamujura, Bernard Lubwama, Solome Okware, Pamela Kajumba
Original abstract (English)
Volume 32, Number 10—October 2026 Synopsis Early Action Review of Detection, Notification, and Response Timeliness during Cross-Border Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak, Uganda, 2026 Suggested citation for this article Abstract Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD), an Ebola virus species with no licensed vaccine or therapeutic, reemerged in May 2026 as a cross-border outbreak in Uganda and the Democratic Republic of the Congo. During a 2-day workshop, July 8–9, 2026, we conducted an early action review of the outbreak response using the 7-1-7 framework (7 days to detect, 1 day to notify, 7 days to complete early response actions) to assess timeliness and identify bottlenecks and enablers across 9 response pillars. WHO recommends that an early action review (EAR), an agile, structured methodology for assessing timeliness and identifying bottlenecks and enablers using the 7-1-7 tool, be conducted for all outbreaks of public health significance (22). In this article, we present the process, results, and lessons learned from that review. Study Design and Setting We conducted a retrospective, multipillar programmatic review using WHO’s standardized EAR methodology and 7-1-7 assessment tool (22). Disclaimer: Early release articles are not considered as final versions. Any changes will be reflected in the online version in the month the article is officially released. Uganda declared its outbreak on May 15, 2026; by July 8, the country had recorded 20 confirmed cases (15 imported, 5 locally transmitted) and a case-fatality rate of 15%. Uganda met all 3 targets: detection in 6 days, notification in <1 day, and response completion in 2 days. Low clinical suspicion, cross-border data-sharing gaps, fragmented digital systems, and delayed community engagement were common bottlenecks; st...
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the detection, notification, and response timeliness during a cross-border Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak in Uganda and the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) in 2026. The central question is whether the outbreak response met the 7-1-7 framework targets for timely detection, notification, and response actions.
Evidence base and method
The evidence base includes a 2-day workshop conducted on July 8–9, 2026, using the 7-1-7 framework to assess outbreak response timeliness. The review evaluates performance across 9 response pillars and identifies bottlenecks and enablers. The methodology is based on a systematic review of outbreak response data and stakeholder input.
Consensus and conclusions
Uganda met all three 7-1-7 targets: detection within 6 days, notification within <1 day, and response completion within 2 days. The review highlights the cross-border nature of the outbreak, with 15 of 20 confirmed cases imported from the DRC. It also identifies key enablers such as strong leadership and coordination structures, and bottlenecks like low clinical suspicion and fragmented digital systems.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that reported case-fatality rates for BVD are generally lower than for Zaire ebolavirus. However, there is limited evidence on the effectiveness of cross-border surveillance and response strategies. Additionally, the review identifies gaps in data-sharing between countries and the need for improved community engagement.
Research and practice implications
The review emphasizes the need for sustained joint surveillance and coordinated screening along the Uganda–DRC corridor. It also highlights the importance of strengthening digital systems, improving clinical suspicion, and enhancing community engagement to prevent future outbreaks. Candidate BVD-specific vaccines and therapeutics are undergoing WHO-led prioritization, which may inform future public health strategies.

Comprehensive Thermodynamic Re-evaluation of Ebola Virus Pathogenesis: Interdisciplinary Mechanistic Solutions via the ELMAS’s Theory of Thermodynamics and the 5th Law of Thermodynamics

Comprehensive Thermodynamic Re-evaluation of Ebola Virus Pathogenesis: Interdisciplinary Mechanistic Solutions via the ELMAS’s Theory of Thermodynamics and the 5th Law of Thermodynamics
作者： Emin Taner ELMAS
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) presents an extraordinary clinical challenge characterized by systemic physiological collapse, uncontrolled cytokine storms, hyperpyrexia, and devastating multi-organ failure. This study introduces a profound paradigm shift by modeling EVD pathogenesis from a mechanical and biomedical engineering perspective, utilizing the foundational principles of the ELMAS Thermodynamic Theory and the newly operationalized 5th Law of Thermodynamics.
证据基础与方法
该基于矩阵的病理模型的结构和数学有效性通过使用4阶龙格-库塔(RK4)积分方案的10,000次试验计算蒙特卡洛框架进行验证,成功识别出关键转折点阈值(theta_crit),标志着自主稳态生存与不可逆多器官死亡之间的界限。
共识与结论
通过将病毒诱导的细胞熵动力学映射到状态空间媒介矩阵中,我们定义了正生命维持能量媒介(Ep)的病理衰减与破坏性负熵媒介(En)的指数上升之间的关系。该跨学科框架成功地将经典流体力学、亚细胞热生物物理学和临床生命支持相连接。
争议与缺口
传统的病毒学框架严格通过孤立的生化和生物分子途径解释这些致命现象。
科研与实践启示
为了抵消这种能量崩溃,我们提出了三种相互关联的高保真工程干预措施:(1)通过微机电系统(MEMS)生物机器人共振频率分析的超早期 诊断;(2) 使用相变医疗热管与调频 (FM) 纳米机器人药物输送算法配对,实现有针对性的零功率内脏热负荷耗散;(3) 高度先进的集成体外膜氧合 (ECMO)-血液透析热力学回路,具有内联热消毒模块(72°C,持续 0.5 秒),旨在减少游离全身病毒颗粒 (Jvirus),同时保持精确的血流动力学控制。

Comprehensive Thermodynamic Re-evaluation of Ebola Virus Pathogenesis: Interdisciplinary Mechanistic Solutions via the ELMAS’s Theory of Thermodynamics and the 5th Law of Thermodynamics

Authors: Emin Taner ELMAS
Original abstract (English)
Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) presents an extraordinary clinical challenge characterized by systemic physiological collapse, uncontrolled cytokine storms, hyperpyrexia, and devastating multi-organ failure. Traditional virological frameworks interpret these lethal phenomena strictly through isolated biochemical and biomolecular pathways. This study introduces a profound paradigm shift by modeling EVD pathogenesis from a mechanical and biomedical engineering perspective, utilizing the foundational principles of the ELMAS Thermodynamic Theory and the newly operationalized 5th Law of Thermodynamics. Under this approach, the human body is mathematically modeled as an open, highly complex bio-machine operating under vector-based, non-stationary energy and mass transfer states. By mapping viral-induced cellular entropy kinetics through state-space vector matrices, we define the pathological decay of positive life-sustaining energy vectors (Ep) against the exponential rise of destructive negative entropy vectors (En). To counteract this energetic collapse, we present three interconnected, high-fidelity engineering interventions: (1) Ultra-early diagnosis via Micro-Electro-Mechanical Systems (MEMS) bio-robotic resonance frequency analysis; (2) Targeted, zero-power visceral thermal load dissipation using phase-change medical heat pipes paired with a frequency-modulated (FM) nanorobot drug delivery algorithm; and (3) A highly advanced, integrated extracorporeal membrane oxygenation (ECMO)-hemodialysis thermodynamic loop featuring an inline thermal disinfection module (72°C for 0.5s) designed to reduce free systemic viral particles (Jvirus) while maintaining precise hemodynamic control. The structural and mathematical validity of this matrix-driven pathological model is verified through a 10,000-trial computational Monte Carlo framework using a 4th-Order Runge-Kutta (RK4) integration scheme, successfully identifying the critical tipping point threshold (theta_crit) that marks the boundary between autonomous homeostatic survival and irreversible multi-organ death. This interdisciplinary framework successfully bridges classical fluid mechanics, sub-cellular thermal biophysics, and clinical life support.[1-96]
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) presents an extraordinary clinical challenge characterized by systemic physiological collapse, uncontrolled cytokine storms, hyperpyrexia, and devastating multi-organ failure. This study introduces a profound paradigm shift by modeling EVD pathogenesis from a mechanical and biomedical engineering perspective, utilizing the foundational principles of the ELMAS Thermodynamic Theory and the newly operationalized 5th Law of Thermodynamics.
Evidence base and method
The structural and mathematical validity of this matrix-driven pathological model is verified through a 10,000-trial computational Monte Carlo framework using a 4th-Order Runge-Kutta (RK4) integration scheme, successfully identifying the critical tipping point threshold (theta_crit) that marks the boundary between autonomous homeostatic survival and irreversible multi-organ death.
Consensus and conclusions
By mapping viral-induced cellular entropy kinetics through state-space vector matrices, we define the pathological decay of positive life-sustaining energy vectors (Ep) against the exponential rise of destructive negative entropy vectors (En). This interdisciplinary framework successfully bridges classical fluid mechanics, sub-cellular thermal biophysics, and clinical life support.
Controversies and gaps
Traditional virological frameworks interpret these lethal phenomena strictly through isolated biochemical and biomolecular pathways.
Research and practice implications
To counteract this energetic collapse, we present three interconnected, high-fidelity engineering interventions: (1) Ultra-early diagnosis via Micro-Electro-Mechanical Systems (MEMS) bio-robotic resonance frequency analysis; (2) Targeted, zero-power visceral thermal load dissipation using phase-change medical heat pipes paired with a frequency-modulated (FM) nanorobot drug delivery algorithm; and (3) A highly advanced, integrated extracorporeal membrane oxygenation (ECMO)-hemodialysis thermodynamic loop featuring an inline thermal disinfection module (72°C for 0.5s) designed to reduce free systemic viral particles (Jvirus) while maintaining precise hemodynamic control.

恐惧、耻辱和不确定性:埃博拉对乌干达西部本迪布焦的幸存者、受影响家庭和社区的短期和长期影响。

Fear, stigma and Uncertainty: The short and long-term effects of Ebola on survivors, affected families, and community in Bundibugyo, Western Uganda.
作者： Shamilah Namusisi, Jacinta Mukulu Waila, Sarah J. Hoffman, Cheryl Roberston, Katey Pelican, Michael Mahero
摘要中文翻译2014年,乌干达被确定为埃博拉病毒病(EVD)高风险国家,自2000年以来记录了一系列暴发。2007年,本迪布焦地区的第二次暴发导致149例报告病例和37例埃博拉确诊死亡。通过 暴发 响应,发现了 埃博拉病毒(本迪布焦埃博拉病毒)的新毒株。尽管人们对埃博拉病毒的性质(包括疾病体征和症状、传播 和管理)了解很多,但对该疾病对社区的短期和长期社会文化影响的了解有限。2007 年 暴发 事件发生 10 年后,研究小组在本迪布焦区进行了一项重点民族志研究。数据收集包括对档案数据的审查、参与观察、现场记录以及对幸存者和受影响家庭的 19 次深度访谈。结果强调了 暴发 造成的短期和长期的社会、文化和经济破坏。我们通过生态健康框架解释了研究结果,重点是潜在的耻辱加剧了 暴发 的有害长期影响。受影响的妇女,特别是寡妇,经历了社会排斥和经济压力,并承认她们的孤儿失去了过上更好生活的机会。由于对 2007 年 暴发 来源的猜测,人们对埃博拉病毒可能复发的恐惧日益加深,导致种族紧张局势。幸存者报告了不同程度的持续健康影响,包括视力受损和全身虚弱。社区成员报告了寻求健康行为的积极变化。卫生保健工作者描述了对埃博拉早期临床症状的高度警惕,这是社区一级早期 暴发 检测的关键因素。我们的研究结果可以为未来的研究、埃博拉应对和恢复干预措施提供信息,特别是那些针对社区重新融入和减轻与幸存者相关的恐惧和耻辱的干预措施。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究重点关注 埃博拉病毒病 (EVD) 对乌干达西部本迪布焦的幸存者、受影响家庭和社区的社会文化影响,特别考察了社会排斥、经济压力和对复发的恐惧等短期和长期影响。核心问题是这些影响在 暴发 后如何在社区中体现和持续。
证据基础与方法
该审查基于 2007 年 Bundibugyo 暴发 10 年后进行的重点民族志研究。数据收集包括对档案数据的审查、参与观察、现场记录以及对幸存者和受影响家庭的 19 次深度访谈。该研究不报告荟萃分析或汇总估计,而是依靠定性方法通过生态健康框架来解释研究结果。
共识与结论
研究得出的结论是,2007 年 Bundibugyo 暴发 造成了严重的短期和长期社会、文化和经济破坏。幸存者报告了持续的健康影响,例如视力受损和全身虚弱,而社区成员则注意到寻求健康行为的积极变化。生态健康框架强调了耻辱如何加剧了 暴发 的长期后果。
争议与缺口
证据表明,人们对埃博拉病毒的社会文化影响的了解有限,尤其是长期影响。对于这些影响的程度或如何系统地衡量这些影响尚未达成共识。研究还指出,对再次发生的恐惧导致了种族紧张局势,但这些紧张局势的根本原因尚未得到充分探讨。
科研与实践启示
研究结果表明,未来的研究和恢复干预措施需要重点关注社区重新融入、解决耻辱问题以及减轻与埃博拉幸存者相关的恐惧。这些影响与改善公共卫生应对措施和长期支持受影响社区息息相关。

Fear, stigma and Uncertainty: The short and long-term effects of Ebola on survivors, affected families, and community in Bundibugyo, Western Uganda.

Authors: Shamilah Namusisi, Jacinta Mukulu Waila, Sarah J. Hoffman, Cheryl Roberston, Katey Pelican, Michael Mahero
Original abstract (English)
In 2014, Uganda was identified as a high-risk country for Ebola Virus Disease (EVD), with a series of outbreaks recorded since 2000. In 2007, the second outbreak in Bundibugyo district resulted in 149 reported cases and 37 confirmed Ebola deaths. Through the outbreak response, a new strain of the Ebola virus ( Bundibugyo ebolavirus) was discovered. Although much is known about the nature of Ebola, including disease signs and symptoms, transmission and management, there is limited understanding of the short and long-term sociocultural impacts of the disease in communities. The study team conducted a focused ethnography in Bundibugyo District 10 years after the 2007 outbreak. Data collection included a review of archival data, participant observation, field notes and 19 in-depth interviews with survivors and affected families. Results underscored short and long term social, cultural and economic disruptions caused by the outbreak. We interpreted findings through an Eco-health framework with an emphasis on ways that underlying stigma accentuated detrimental long-term effects of the outbreak. Affected women, particularly widows, experienced social exclusion, and economic strain, and acknowledged loss of opportunity for a better life for their orphaned children. Deepening fear of the possible recurrence of Ebola resulted in ethnic tension driven by speculations on the 2007 outbreak source. Survivors reported varying persistent health effects including impaired vision and general body weakness. Community members reported positive changes in health seeking behaviors. Health care workers described high levels of alert for early clinical signs of Ebola, a critical factor for early outbreak detection at the community level. Our findings can inform future research, Ebola response, and recovery interventions, particularly those targeting community re-integration and mitigation of the fear and stigma associated with survivorship.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focuses on the sociocultural impacts of Ebola Virus Disease (EVD) on survivors, affected families, and communities in Bundibugyo, Western Uganda, particularly examining short and long-term effects such as social exclusion, economic strain, and fear of recurrence. The central question is how these impacts manifest and persist in the community post-outbreak.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on a focused ethnography conducted 10 years after the 2007 Bundibugyo outbreak. Data collection involved a review of archival data, participant observation, field notes, and 19 in-depth interviews with survivors and affected families. The study does not report on meta-analysis or pooled estimates but relies on qualitative methods to interpret findings through an Eco-health framework.
Consensus and conclusions
The study concludes that the 2007 Bundibugyo outbreak caused significant short and long-term social, cultural, and economic disruptions. Survivors reported persistent health effects such as impaired vision and general body weakness, while community members noted positive changes in health-seeking behaviors. The Eco-health framework highlights how stigma exacerbated the long-term consequences of the outbreak.
Controversies and gaps
The evidence indicates limited understanding of the sociocultural impacts of Ebola, particularly in the long-term. There is no consensus on the extent of these impacts or how they can be systematically measured. The study also notes that fear of recurrence led to ethnic tensions, but the underlying causes of these tensions are not fully explored.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest the need for future research and recovery interventions that focus on community reintegration, addressing stigma, and mitigating fear associated with Ebola survivorship. These implications are tied to improving public health responses and supporting affected communities in the long term.

盲点的代价:2025 年美国国际开发署关闭如何削弱全球 暴发 检测

The cost of blind spots: How the 2025 USAID closure weakened global outbreak detection
作者： Alex Mirugwe
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该审查重点关注 2025 年美国国际开发署关闭对全球 暴发 检测的影响,特别是在 埃博拉病毒 和相关实体的背景下。核心问题是美国国际开发署健康保障计划的减少如何影响 2026 年本迪布焦埃博拉 暴发 的早期发现和应对。
证据基础与方法
该审查基于有关 2025 年美国国际开发署关闭、其对项目的影响以及随后的 2026 年 Bundibugyo 埃博拉 暴发 的一般背景信息。它不报告特定数据库、检索日期、资格标准或研究的荟萃分析。证据主要是叙述性和描述性的,没有提及系统评价方法或研究计数。
共识与结论
美国国际开发署 2025 年的关闭可能导致 暴发 检测和响应的延迟,特别是在刚果民主共和国。它强调了美国国际开发署的 监测 网络在快速检测和遏制 暴发 方面的重要性,特别是在偏远和受冲突影响的地区。Bundibugyo 病毒缺乏经批准的 疫苗 或 治疗,被认为是管理 暴发 挑战的关键因素。
争议与缺口
该审查承认,美国国际开发署关闭对 暴发 检测的具体影响尚未定量确定。它指出,没有关于哪些 监测 活动、实验室合同或现场工作人员职位因政策变化而中断的已发布数据。此外,审查指出,将检测延迟仅归因于美国国际开发署的关闭,而不是归因于 暴发 的偏远和受冲突影响的环境,需要更详细的活动级别 暴发 数据,但这些数据尚不可用。
科研与实践启示
审查建议未来的研究应侧重于量化 USAID 的 监测 项目对 暴发 检测和响应的具体贡献。它还强调需要改进有关政策变化对全球卫生安全影响的数据收集和透明度。实际上,它强调了在面临新发传染病风险的地区维持强大的 监测 基础设施的重要性。

The cost of blind spots: How the 2025 USAID closure weakened global outbreak detection

Authors: Alex Mirugwe
Original abstract (English)
In early 2025, shortly after assuming office, the Trump administration ordered a comprehensive review of U.S. This led to an immediate pause on new obligations and disbursements, followed by stop-work orders and the termination of approximately 83 percent of USAID's programs. By mid-2025, the agency was effectively dismantled, with remaining functions absorbed into the Department of State amid substantial staff reductions and contract cancellations. The administration cited the need to eliminate waste, refocus on core national interests, and ensure greater accountability and efficiency in foreign aid spending (NPR, 2025;Poddar & Rao, 2025).For decades, USAID had played a central role in building and sustaining global health security. It funded surveillance networks in zoonotic hotspots, supported laboratory strengthening, trained frontline health workers, and enabled rapid sample transport and testing. These investments, often implemented in partnership with the CDC, WHO, and local governments, had repeatedly demonstrated their value by shortening outbreak detection and response times in challenging environments (Oxfam America, 2026). A single flagship activity, USAID's STRIDES program, represented an estimated $245 million investment in partner-country disease-detection capacity. Independent government evaluations had previously found that the impact of such surveillance investments on outbreak detection was rarely assessed in the peerreviewed literature, a gap that also limits precise attribution of the 2026 outbreak delays discussed below (Ilesanmi & Afolabi, 2022).The 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola Outbreak In mid-May 2026, health authorities in the Democratic Republic of the Congo confirmed an outbreak of Ebola disease caused by the Bundibugyo virus in Ituri Province. As of the WHO's initial Disease Outbreak News notice on May 17, 2026, the outbreak had been linked to roughly 12 laboratory-confirmed cases and around 500 suspected cases. WHO's subsequent weekly situation reports revised these figures as retrospective testing cleared a backlog of samples: 85 confirmed cases and 746 cumulative suspected cases, including 176 deaths among suspected cases, by May 21, and 550 confirmed cases across Ituri, North Kivu, and South Kivu provinces by June 7 (World Health Organization Regional Office for Africa, 2026c, 2026d). This progression, in which suspected cases are successively laboratory-confirmed or excluded, accounts for the wide ranges reported in early coverage rather than reflecting inconsistent case counting. The virus also spread to Uganda, where 20 confirmed cases and 2 deaths linked to travelers from the DRC had been recorded by mid-July, with no new cases reported since June 21. On May 17, the WHO declared the event a Public Health Emergency of International Concern (World Health Organization, 2026a). This strain, rarer than the more familiar Zaire ebolavirus, lacks approved vaccines or specific treatments, complicating containment. The outbreak likely circulated for several weeks before confirmation, gaining momentum amid conflict, population displacement, and strained local health systems. Initial testing protocols focused on more common strains may have contributed to the delay in recognition (Science, 2026).Public health experts have noted that the erosion of USAID-supported networks likely played a role in the slower early response. Prior to 2025, U.S.-backed programs in eastern DRC provided critical community surveillance, laboratory logistics, and rapid reporting capabilities that had helped contain previous outbreaks more swiftly. With many of these disrupted, local authorities faced greater challenges in detecting and verifying the unusual cluster of severe illnesses in a remote, insecure region. While zoonotic spillover from animal reservoirs and ongoing conflict remain the primary drivers of emergence, the loss of established early-warning infrastructure may have contributed to wider transmission before full international mobilization (KFF, 2025;Harvard T.H. Chan School of Public Health, 2025). This account remains circumstantial: the manuscript is not aware of published data quantifying which specific surveillance activities, laboratory contracts, or field staff positions in eastern DRC were interrupted by USAID's restructuring, nor of a formal before-after comparison of detection capacity in the affected health zones. For context, a peer-reviewed analysis of outbreaks reported to WHO's African Region between 2017 and 2019 found a median time to detection of 8 days (interquartile range 2-28), with detection times improving year over year (Impouma et al., 2020). The multi-week interval before the Bundibugyo outbreak was confirmed sits well outside that historical range, though attributing this specific delay to the loss of any single donor's programs, rather than to the conflict-affected and remote setting in which the outbreak emerged, would require activity-level surveillance data that are not yet publicly available.The concurrent 2026 events reveal the practical consequences of reduced surveillance capacity. Critics, including former officials and global health analysts, had warned that abrupt cuts would create blind spots in precisely the regions where new threats often emerge first. Projections indicated not only setbacks in controlling endemic diseases but also slower detection of emerging ones. In an interconnected world, delays in one location can lead to importations and wider risks, as evidenced by travel-related cases in this outbreak (Auwal, 2025).Defenders of the policy emphasize that many programs suffered from inefficiencies and that remaining functions could be managed more effectively under the State Department. They argue that outbreaks stem primarily from local ecological and social factors, and that other actors, such as the Africa CDC and WHO, are actively responding. These points are valid. Foreign assistance has long required scrutiny for sustainability and results, and diversified funding can reduce dependency on any single donor. In this very outbreak, the Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention (Africa CDC) declared a Public Health Emergency of Continental Security and launched a joint response plan with WHO, illustrating the growing capacity of regional institutions to lead health-security responses (Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention, 2026;Evaborhene, 2026). This regional stepping-up should be read against a backdrop of structural donor dependence that predates the 2025 closure. Sub-Saharan African governments devoted only around 7.2% of national budgets to health in recent years, compared with an average of 12.4% in other regions, while development assistance for health is projected to grow more slowly than the economies it supports (Nonvignon et al., 2024). A systematic review of donor-funded health programs across Africa found that fewer than four in five continued to operate beyond their original funding period, underscoring that reliance on any single external donor, including USAID, was already a recognized vulnerability before this closure (Ilesanmi & Afolabi, 2022). Strengthening domestic financing and regional institutions such as Africa CDC is therefore not merely a response to USAID's withdrawal but a longer-standing priority that this episode makes more urgent (Poddar & Rao, 2025).Nevertheless, historical performance data show that U.S.-supported systems provided measurable speed and scale in early detection that partners have not fully replaced in the short term. Early containment remains far less costly than large-scale responses or potential pandemic-level events.A pragmatic path forward involves restoring targeted surveillance and laboratory capacities in high-risk zones through hybrid models that combine bilateral efforts with multilateral partners. Greater emphasis on building resilient, locally owned systems, alongside rigorous accountability metrics, could address efficiency concerns while preserving essential earlywarning functions. Treating global health security as an enduring national interest, rather than subject to abrupt shifts, would better protect both humanitarian goals and U.S. security.Outbreaks will continue to arise from the interplay of nature, human behavior, and environmental pressures. The question is whether societies confront them with robust detection systems or with avoidable gaps in visibility. The events of May 2026 suggest that preserving smart, accountable surveillance infrastructure is not merely an expense but a necessary investment in collective safety.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the impact of the 2025 USAID closure on global outbreak detection, particularly in the context of Ebola viruses and related entities. The central question is how the reduction in USAID's health security programs affected the early detection and response to the 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola Outbreak.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on general background information about the 2025 USAID closure, its effects on programs, and the subsequent 2026 Bundibugyo Ebola Outbreak. It does not report on specific databases, search dates, eligibility criteria, or a meta-analysis of studies. The evidence is primarily narrative and descriptive, with no mention of systematic review methods or study counts.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that the 2025 USAID closure likely contributed to delays in outbreak detection and response, particularly in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. It highlights the importance of USAID's surveillance networks in enabling rapid detection and containment of outbreaks, especially in remote and conflict-affected areas. The Bundibugyo virus, which lacks approved vaccines or treatments, was identified as a key factor in the challenges of managing the outbreak.
Controversies and gaps
The review acknowledges that the specific impact of USAID's closure on outbreak detection is not quantitatively established. It notes that there is no published data on which surveillance activities, laboratory contracts, or field staff positions were disrupted by the policy change. Additionally, the review points out that attributing the delay in detection solely to USAID's closure, rather than to the outbreak's remote and conflict-affected setting, requires more detailed activity-level surveillance data that are not yet available.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that future research should focus on quantifying the specific contributions of USAID's surveillance programs to outbreak detection and response. It also emphasizes the need for improved data collection and transparency regarding the impact of policy changes on global health security. Practically, it underscores the importance of maintaining robust surveillance infrastructure in regions at risk for emerging infectious diseases.

气候变化背景下苏丹水电和灌溉开发的综合水文和经济评估

Integrated hydrological and economic assessment of hydropower and irrigation development in Sudan under climate change

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

IOM:多国 Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒病 暴发 - 第 16 号情况报告(2026 年 8 月 24 日至 30 日) - Consultaelectoral.onpe.gob.pe

IOM: Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak - Situation Report No. 16 (24-30 August 2026) - consultaelectoral.onpe.gob.pe
新闻简报The IOM Situation Report No. 16 (24-30 August 2026) highlights a multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease (BVD) outbreak, with over 5,900 confirmed cases and more than 2,800 confirmed deaths reported in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) as of 28 August 2026. This represents an 82 per cent increase in cases and a 99 per cent increase in deaths compared to July 2026. The outbreak is unfolding in eastern DRC, where conflict, displacement, and health risks converge, placing internally displaced persons (IDPs) and mobile populations at particular risk. IOM has scaled up its response, particularly at points of entry (PoEs), points of control (PoCs), and displacement sites, to enhance detection, prevention, and response efforts. The spread into Bas-Uélé Province underscores the need for addressing mobility dimensions to prevent regional spillover into the Central African Republic (CAR) and South Sudan. Contact tracing is weak in Bas-Uélé, with only 18.9 per cent effectiveness as per government sources. The report emphasizes the importance of continued disease surveillance and monitoring to ensure a complete epidemiological picture.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该情况报告于2026年9月4日发布,引用的数据涵盖2026年8月24日至30日期间。暴发正在进行中,2026年7月至8月期间报告的病例和死亡人数有所增加。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在刚果民主共和国 (DRC),特别是刚果民主共和国东部,包括伊图里省和北基伍省。它还影响到乌干达和肯尼亚等邻国。截至 2026 年 8 月 28 日,报告确诊病例超过 5,900 例,死亡病例超过 2,800 例,其中大量病例涉及境内流离失所者 (IDP) 和流动人口。
事件
据报道,多国Bundibugyo 埃博拉病毒病(BVD)ZX​​QTERMXQXZ,刚果民主共和国及其邻国出现确诊病例和死亡。刚果民主共和国卫生部报告了这些数字,国际移民组织正在通过加强卫生运营和 暴发 积极响应 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
The outbreak has seen an 82 per cent increase in cases and a 99 per cent increase in deaths between July and August 2026. The spread into Bas-Uélé Province highlights the risk of regional spillover into the Central African Republic (CAR) and South Sudan. Contact tracing is weak in Bas-Uélé, with only 18.9 per cent effectiveness as per government sources.
应对、状态与不确定性
国际移民组织(IOM)加大了应对力度,重点关注入境点(PoE)、控制点(PoC)和流离失所地点。应对措施包括加强受影响国家之间的增援支持、行动能力和协调。暴发 正在进行中,情况仍在调查中,需要继续进行疾病 监测 和监测。
A high priorityPublic-health news

IOM: Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak - Situation Report No. 16 (24-30 August 2026) - consultaelectoral.onpe.gob.pe

News Brief
The IOM Situation Report No. 16 (24-30 August 2026) highlights a multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease (BVD) outbreak, with over 5,900 confirmed cases and more than 2,800 confirmed deaths reported in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) as of 28 August 2026. This represents an 82 per cent increase in cases and a 99 per cent increase in deaths compared to July 2026. The outbreak is unfolding in eastern DRC, where conflict, displacement, and health risks converge, placing internally displaced persons (IDPs) and mobile populations at particular risk. IOM has scaled up its response, particularly at points of entry (PoEs), points of control (PoCs), and displacement sites, to enhance detection, prevention, and response efforts. The spread into Bas-Uélé Province underscores the need for addressing mobility dimensions to prevent regional spillover into the Central African Republic (CAR) and South Sudan. Contact tracing is weak in Bas-Uélé, with only 18.9 per cent effectiveness as per government sources. The report emphasizes the importance of continued disease surveillance and monitoring to ensure a complete epidemiological picture.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Help Log in ReliefWeb Search Content Search What are you looking for? Search|t Updates Countries Disasters Organizations Topics Jobs Training Informing humanitarians worldwide 24/7 — a service provided by UN OCHA DR Congo + 7 more IOM: Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak - Situation Report No. 16 (24-30 August 2026) Format Situation Report Source IOM Posted 4 Sep 2026 Originally published 4 Sep 2026 Origin View original Attachments Download Report (PDF | 2.15 MB) SITUATION OVERVIEW According to the Ministry of Health of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), over 5,900 confirmed cases of Ebola disease caused by the Bundibugyo virus disease (BVD) had been reported as of 28 August, including more than 2,800 confirmed deaths. This represents an 82 per cent increase in cases reported between July and August and a 99 per cent increase in the number of deaths reported over the same period. The outbreak is unfolding against the backdrop of a longstanding triple crisis in eastern DRC, where conflict, displacement and health risks converge, leaving internally displaced persons (IDPs) and mobile populations at particular risk. Over 50 IOM-managed IDP sites in eastern DRC are located in areas of Ituri and North Kivu impacted by the outbreak. In such contexts, inadequate water, sanitation and hygiene (WASH), health and shelter services accelerate risks of disease transmission and compound vulnerabilities, impacting the safety and protection of displaced, mobile and host communities. In turn, mobility continues to shape disease transmission. Recent data from IOM’s Displacement Tracking Matrix (DTM) highlights that one in two recorded journeys between 17 – 23 August started in an active transmission zone, with 4.5 per cent of the flows originating from active transmission zones leaving Ituri to other provinces within DRC, as well as to neighbouring countries of Uganda and Kenya. At the same time, data reported at points of control (PoCs) indicates a total of 165 case fatalities intercepted at PoCs – highlighting the continued importance of expanding health operations across DRC to effectively detect, prevent and respond to potential new cases. The spread of the outbreak into Bas-Uélé Province underscores the continued need to comprehensively address the mobility dimensions of the ongoing public health response to prevent regional spillover into the Central African Republic (CAR) and South Sudan. Contact tracing is particularly weak for Bas-Uélé (18.9 per cent as per government sources), underscoring the urgent need for continued disease surveillance and monitoring to ensure a complete epidemiological picture. In line with the Inter-Agency Standing Committee (IASC) systemwide scale-up activation for infectious disease events, IOM has scaled up its response, particularly at points of entry (PoEs), PoCs and in displacement sites, enabling enhanced surge support, operational capacity and coordination across affected countries. IOM’s response continues to focus on Priority 1a countries, DRC and Uganda, where outbreak response and enhanced preparedness activities remain ongoing, and Priority 1b countries, Burundi, Rwanda and South Sudan, which face an elevated risk of crossborder transmission. Preparedness interventions are also being implemented in other at-risk countries across the region, including Priority 2 countries, Angola, CAR, Ethiopia, Kenya, the Republic of the Congo, the United Republic of Tanzania and Zambia, to strengthen readiness and enhance regional health security, while supporting continent-wide efforts, particularly as co-lead of the PoE technical working group at the continental level. Related Content DR Congo + 2 more Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak: Democratic Republic of the Congo | Uganda Weekly External Situation Report 17, Data as of 06 September 2026 DR Congo + 9 more IOM: Multi-Country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease Outbreak - Situation Report No. 15 (17-23 August 2026) DR Congo + 1 more Regional Situation Report: Ebola Bundibugyo Virus Disease Outbreak 1 - 31 July 2026 DR Congo + 1 more Ebola Bundibugyo virus disease outbreak: Democratic Republic of the Congo | Uganda Weekly External Situation Report 15, Data as of 23 August 2026
View five news elements
Time
The situation report was published on 4 September 2026, and the data referenced covers the period from 24-30 August 2026. The outbreak is ongoing, with increased cases and deaths reported between July and August 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak is occurring in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), specifically in eastern DRC, including Ituri and North Kivu provinces. It also affects neighboring countries such as Uganda and Kenya. Over 5,900 confirmed cases and more than 2,800 confirmed deaths were reported as of 28 August 2026, with a significant number of cases involving internally displaced persons (IDPs) and mobile populations.
Event
A multi-country Bundibugyo Ebola Virus Disease (BVD) outbreak is reported, with confirmed cases and deaths in the DRC and neighboring countries. The Ministry of Health of the DRC reported these figures, and the IOM is actively responding to the outbreak through enhanced health operations and surveillance.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak has seen an 82 per cent increase in cases and a 99 per cent increase in deaths between July and August 2026. The spread into Bas-Uélé Province highlights the risk of regional spillover into the Central African Republic (CAR) and South Sudan. Contact tracing is weak in Bas-Uélé, with only 18.9 per cent effectiveness as per government sources.
Response, status and uncertainty
IOM has scaled up its response, focusing on points of entry (PoEs), points of control (PoCs), and displacement sites. The response includes enhanced surge support, operational capacity, and coordination across affected countries. The outbreak is ongoing, and the situation remains under investigation with a need for continued disease surveillance and monitoring.

刚果民主共和国埃博拉病毒病暴发 - 欧洲疾病预防控制中心(ECDC)

Ebola disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo - European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC)
新闻简报欧洲疾病预防控制中心(ECDC)报告称,刚果民主共和国(DRC)和乌干达发生了大规模埃博拉病毒病暴发。虽然确切的病例和死亡人数未被具体说明,但ECDC已发布沟通材料和评估报告以监测局势。该机构强调,欧盟/欧洲经济区(EEA)居民感染的可能性非常低,表明欧洲面临的风险有限。此次暴发仍在持续,最新更新于2026年9月4日发布。ECDC正在积极评估风险并提供指导,但相关信息仍有限,且此次暴发的状态仍在调查中。本报告强调了欧洲在应对此次暴发潜在威胁方面需要保持持续警惕和准备。
查看新闻五要素
时间
此次暴发仍在持续,最新更新于2026年9月4日发布。ECDC自至少2026年起一直在监测该情况。
地点与对象
此次暴发发生在刚果民主共和国(DRC)和乌干达。受影响的人群包括人类和可能的动物,但未提供具体细节。
事件
据报道,刚果民主共和国和乌干达发生了埃博拉病毒病暴发。ECDC已发布有关此次暴发的沟通材料和评估报告,但事件的具体细节尚未明确确认。
规模、影响与风险
此次暴发被描述为大规模,但未提供具体的病例或死亡人数。ECDC评估认为,欧盟/欧洲经济区(EEA)居民感染的可能性非常低,表明欧洲面临的风险有限。
应对、状态与不确定性
ECDC正在积极评估风险并为欧洲提供指导。然而,信息仍然有限,暴发 的状态正在接受调查。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Ebola disease outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo - European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC)

News Brief
The European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) has reported a large outbreak of Ebola disease in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and Uganda. While the exact number of cases and deaths is not specified, the ECDC has issued communication materials and assessments to monitor the situation. The agency emphasizes that the likelihood of infection for people in the EU/EEA is very low, indicating limited risk to Europe. The outbreak is ongoing, with the latest updates published on 4 September 2026. The ECDC is actively assessing risks and providing guidance, but the information remains limited, and the status of the outbreak is under investigation. This report highlights the need for continued vigilance and preparedness in Europe to address potential threats from the outbreak.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

埃博拉 暴发:现状 - 疾病控制与预防中心 |疾病预防控制中心 (.gov)

Ebola Outbreak: Current Situation - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
新闻简报CDC 正在应对刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病毒 暴发。此次 暴发 目前是有记录以来的第二大疫情,在响应启动后 40 天内确诊病例数已超过 1,000 例。这是刚果民主共和国第17个暴发,上韦莱省、伊图里省、北基伍省、南基伍省和乔波省都有确诊病例。由于这个暴发,美国尚未确诊任何病例,传播到美国的风险被认为非常低。由于卫生基础设施有限、持续的冲突和安全问题,应对工作面临挑战。针对抵达后 21 天内去过刚果民主共和国或乌干达的美国旅客实施旅行限制和加强筛查。疾病预防控制中心建议避免前往伊图里省和北基伍省,并对其他地区采取预防措施。随着调查的继续,案件数量可能会发生变化。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发目前仍在进行中,最新数据截止至2026-09-07。暴发在响应启动后40天内超过了1,000例确诊病例,南基伍省的最后报告病例于6月21日被确认。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在刚果民主共和国 (DRC) 的偏远地区,特别是上韦莱省、伊图里省、北基伍省(北基伍省)、南基伍省(南基伍省)和乔波省。公众和旅行者也被提到由于潜在的进口而面临风险。
事件
CDC 正在应对刚果民主共和国由本迪布焦病毒引起的埃博拉病毒 暴发。暴发 的传播速度比之前的传播速度要快得多,目前是有记录以来的第二大传播速度。美国尚未因该 暴发 确诊任何病例。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 响应启动后 40 天内确诊病例数已超过 1,000 例。这是刚果民主共和国的第 17 个 暴发。美国公众和旅行者面临的风险仍然很低,埃博拉病毒传播到美国的可能性被认为非常低。如果在美国确诊病例,由于公共卫生系统和感染控制措施的实力,传播风险仍然较低。
应对、状态与不确定性
由于医疗基础设施有限、持续的冲突以及对医护人员的暴力行为,应对暴发的行动面临挑战。对额外病例的调查仍在进行中,病例数量预计会有变化。为降低向美国输入风险,已实施旅行限制和加强筛查措施。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Ebola Outbreak: Current Situation - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
The CDC is addressing an Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) caused by the Bundibugyo virus. This outbreak, now the second-largest on record, has surpassed 1,000 confirmed cases within 40 days of response activation. It is the 17th outbreak in the DRC, with cases confirmed in Haut-Uele, Ituri, Nord-Kivu, Sud-Kivu, and Tshopo provinces. No cases have been confirmed in the U.S. due to this outbreak, and the risk of spreading to the U.S. is considered very low. The response faces challenges due to limited health infrastructure, ongoing conflict, and security issues. Travel restrictions and enhanced screening are in place for U.S. travelers who have been in the DRC or Uganda within 21 days of arrival. The CDC recommends avoiding travel to Ituri and Nord-Kivu provinces and taking precautions for other areas. Case counts are subject to change as investigations continue.

To date, no cases of Ebola disease have been confirmed in the United States because of this outbreak. The overall risk to the American public and travelers remains low. Outbreak overview Risk to the United States To date, no Ebola cases associated with this outbreak have been reported in the United States. The likelihood of Ebola spreading to the United States is considered very low. If a case was diagnosed in the United States, the risk of Ebola spreading in the United States is also low due to the strength of our public health system and infection control measures. CDC is responding to an outbreak of Ebola in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). This outbreak is spreading substantially faster than previous Ebola outbreaks and is now the second-largest Ebola outbreak on record. The outbreak is caused by Bundibugyo virus, a type of Ebola virus. In DRC, cases have been confirmed in Haut-Uele, Ituri, Nord-Kivu (North Kivu), Sud-Kivu (South Kivu), and Tshopo provinces. This is the 17th outbreak of Ebola in DRC. This Ebola outbreak surpassed 1,000 confirmed cases within 40 days of response activation. In comparison, the 2018 Ebola outbreak in DRC took approximately 235 days to reach more than 1,000 cases. South Kivu has reported no confirmed cases since May 29. Uganda also reported cases of Ebola at the same time DRC declared an outbreak. Uganda reported 21 confirmed and probable cases and 3 deaths. The last reported case there was confirmed June 21. Response challenges The response to this outbreak is challenging due to a number of complex circumstances in the region. Limited health infrastructure, including limited access to healthcare, diagnostic and laboratory services, infection prevention and control supplies, and other basic services make stopping this outbreak more difficult. Ongoing conflict in the area where the outbreak is occurring creates security issues and complicates contact tracing and infection prevention and control. Violence against healthcare workers and shortages of personal protective equipment are leading to healthcare workers becoming infected while treating patients. Frequent population movement and cross-border travel increase the risk of spread to Uganda, South Sudan, Rwanda, and to other areas. Lack of trust in the government and misinformation make communicating about Ebola more difficult. To reduce the risk of Ebola importation into the United States, U.S. entry for travelers who were recently in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) is temporarily restricted. Travelers, including Americans, who have been in DRC within 21 days of their flight will not be allowed to board commercial flights with U.S. destinations. All U.S. citizens and U.S. nationals who have been in DRC should plan to remain outside DRC for 21 days before entering the United States. This includes all passengers with an intermediate stop in DRC or on multi-stop itineraries via DRC regardless of whether they disembark or stay on the airplane. U.S. citizens and U.S. nationals who have been present in Uganda or South Sudan (and not present in DRC) within 21 days of arrival in the United States must only enter through designated airports for enhanced screening. This process is in addition to CDC's renewed August 12 order, under 42 CFR 71.40, that continued the temporary entry restrictions to the United States for certain categories of travelers after they were in DRC, Uganda, or South Sudan. CDC recommends avoiding all travel to Ituri and Nord-Kivu (North Kivu) provinces and avoiding nonessential travel to Haut-Uele and Tshopo provinces in DRC. Travelers to other parts of DRC or Uganda should take enhanced precautions to avoid Ebola exposure and monitor for symptoms while traveling and for 21 days after leaving. Latest data Reported cases This is a rapidly evolving situation, and case counts are subject to change. The DRC, Uganda, and France ministries of health (MOH) report the following: Skip Data Table Data table showing data for the table figure. The DRC MoH does not include probable cases in their official reporting. Suspected cases and suspected deaths in DRC have been temporarily excluded from the count pending ongoing investigations and data standardization. The Uganda MoH does not include suspected cases in their official reporting. Map of affected areas View LargerDownload To date, the Ebola disease outbreak in DRC has been confirmed in Haut-Uele, Ituri, Nord-Kivu (North Kivu), Sud-Kivu (South Kivu), and Tshopo provinces. Cases related to the DRC outbreak also have been reported in Uganda's capital of Kampala. CDC response CDC is working internationally and domestically to respond to this outbreak and prevent Ebola from entering the United States. About 500 people at CDC are involved in responding to the outbreak, including more than 120 people deployed to the affected countries. Keep Reading Ebola Outbreak: What CDC is Doing Information for returning travelers CDC has guidance for people who recently have been in areas affected by this Ebola outbreak, including what to do if you feel sick after travel. View five news elements
Time
The outbreak is currently ongoing, with the latest data as of 2026-09-07. The outbreak surpassed 1,000 confirmed cases within 40 days of response activation, and the last reported case in South Kivu was confirmed June 21.
Location and population
The outbreak is occurring in remote areas of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), specifically in Haut-Uele, Ituri, Nord-Kivu (North Kivu), Sud-Kivu (South Kivu), and Tshopo provinces. public and travelers are also mentioned as being at risk due to potential importation.
Event
CDC is responding to an outbreak of Ebola disease caused by Bundibugyo virus in the DRC. The outbreak is spreading substantially faster than previous ones and is now the second-largest on record. No cases have been confirmed in the United States due to this outbreak.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak has surpassed 1,000 confirmed cases within 40 days of response activation. It is the 17th outbreak in the DRC. The risk to the American public and travelers remains low, and the likelihood of Ebola spreading to the United States is considered very low. If a case was diagnosed in the U.S., the risk of spread would still be low due to the strength of the public health system and infection control measures.
Response, status and uncertainty
The response to the outbreak is challenging due to limited health infrastructure, ongoing conflict, and violence against healthcare workers. Investigation of additional cases is ongoing, and case counts are expected to change. Travel restrictions and enhanced screening are in place to reduce the risk of importation into the U.S.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

刚果民主共和国社区对话建立埃博拉病毒应对信任 - 世界卫生组织 | 非洲区域办事处

Community dialogue building trust in Ebola response in the Democratic Republic of the Congo - WHO | Regional Office for Africa
新闻简报该文章由世界卫生组织(WHO)非洲区域办事处于2026年9月3日发布,讨论了旨在建立刚果民主共和国埃博拉病毒应对信任的社区对话举措。文章强调了与当地社区互动以提高公共卫生措施有效性的必要性。内容还提及其他与埃博拉病毒相关的2026年9月新闻,包括针对不断进化的暴发调整护理措施的努力。然而,文章并未提供具体的病例数、死亡数或暴露人数,表明信息仍处于初步阶段。文章的重点在于健康紧急事件应对的更广泛背景以及世卫组织在支持这些努力中的作用。文章未详细说明暴发的当前状况或任何具体调查结果,暗示仍在进行努力且情况存在不确定性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章于2026-09-03发布。它提到了2026年9月的事件,包括《在刚果民主共和国针对不断进化的埃博拉病毒暴发调整护理措施》和《刚果民主共和国社区对话建立埃博拉病毒应对信任》的相关报道。
地点与对象
文章提到的地点是刚果民主共和国。文章讨论了该地区埃博拉病毒应对措施,重点在于社区参与和建立信任的努力。
事件
文章重点介绍了旨在建立对刚果民主共和国埃博拉应对工作信任的社区对话举措。这是世界卫生组织 (WHO) 的报道,属于专题报道,而非已确认的 暴发 或健康事件。
规模、影响与风险
该文章没有提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。它侧重于卫生应急响应和社区参与的更广泛背景,但没有详细说明埃博拉 暴发 的规模或风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
应对状态被描述为涉及社区对话和建立信任,这是世卫组织更广泛努力的一部分。文章没有具体说明 暴发 的当前状态或任何调查细节,表明该信息是初步的且正在进行中。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Community dialogue building trust in Ebola response in the Democratic Republic of the Congo - WHO | Regional Office for Africa

News Brief
The article, published on September 3, 2026, by the World Health Organization (WHO) Regional Office for Africa, discusses community dialogue initiatives aimed at building trust in the Ebola response in the Democratic Republic of the Congo. It highlights the importance of engaging local communities to enhance the effectiveness of public health measures. The content references other Ebola-related news from September 2026, including efforts to adapt care for an evolving outbreak. However, it does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers, indicating that the information is preliminary. The focus is on the broader context of health emergency responses and the role of WHO in supporting these efforts. The article does not detail the current status of the outbreak or any specific investigation findings, suggesting ongoing efforts and uncertainty in the situation.
